        Idea:
Fix the video editor's OverlayEditor (preview drag/resize) system. Three issues: (1) Moving a clip on the preview changes its aspect ratio/shape when it should maintain dimensions — only corner resize should change size. (2) Need edge dragging for cropping (left/right/top/bottom edges crop the clip) vs corner dragging for resize. (3) Ghost/shadow artifact when dragging clips on the timeline — something visual lingers until drop. Investigate whether these are symptomatic of poor structure in OverlayEditor.tsx and related code, or just incremental fixes. Goal: make preview overlay interactions predictable and clean — move preserves shape, corners resize, edges crop, no visual artifacts.

User notes and answers:
- T3/T4 were completely misdirected at timeline drag code. Refocus entirely on OverlayEditor.tsx preview interactions. The ghost is likely related to the drag preview rendering in OverlayEditor itself — possibly the live drag overlay (lines 327-359) or how bounds are rendered during drag. For crop UX: selected clip shows full uncropped bounds as a dimmed ghost behind the visible cropped area.

  {
    "id": "T1",
    "description": "Fix string-matching bug in OverlayEditor.tsx drag handler (lines 215-232). The `state.mode.includes('e')` checks run unconditionally after the `move` branch, and `'move'.includes('e')` is `true`, causing every move to also apply east-resize. Fix: wrap lines 219-232 in an `else` block so resize logic only runs when `state.mode !== 'move'`.\n\nFile: `src/tools/video-editor/components/PreviewPanel/OverlayEditor.tsx`\n\nBefore:\n```\nif (state.mode === 'move') { nextBounds.x += deltaX; nextBounds.y += deltaY; }\nif (state.mode.includes('e')) { ... }  // BUG: fires during move\n```\nAfter:\n```\nif (state.mode === 'move') {\n  nextBounds.x += deltaX;\n  nextBounds.y += deltaY;\n} else {\n  if (state.mode.includes('e')) { ... }\n  if (state.mode.includes('s')) { ... }\n  if (state.mode.includes('w')) { ... }\n  if (state.mode.includes('n')) { ... }\n}\n```",

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Fix the video editor's OverlayEditor (preview drag/resize) system. Three issues: (1) Moving a clip on the preview changes its aspect ratio/shape when it should maintain dimensions — only corner resize should change size. (2) Need edge dragging for cropping (left/right/top/bottom edges crop the clip) vs corner dragging for resize. (3) Ghost/shadow artifact when dragging clips on the timeline — something visual lingers until drop. Investigate whether these are symptomatic of poor structure in OverlayEditor.tsx and related code, or just incremental fixes. Goal: make preview overlay interactions predictable and clean — move preserves shape, corners resize, edges crop, no visual artifacts.

User notes and answers:
- T3/T4 were completely misdirected at timeline drag code. Refocus entirely on OverlayEditor.tsx preview interactions. The ghost is likely related to the drag preview rendering in OverlayEditor itself — possibly the live drag overlay (lines 327-359) or how bounds are rendered during drag. For crop UX: selected clip shows full uncropped bounds as a dimmed ghost behind the visible cropped area.

  {
    "id": "T1",
    "description": "Fix string-matching bug in OverlayEditor.tsx drag handler (lines 215-232). The `state.mode.includes('e')` checks run unconditionally after the `move` branch, and `'move'.includes('e')` is `true`, causing every move to also apply east-resize. Fix: wrap lines 219-232 in an `else` block so resize logic only runs when `state.mode !== 'move'`.\n\nFile: `src/tools/video-editor/components/PreviewPanel/OverlayEditor.tsx`\n\nBefore:\n```\nif (state.mode === 'move') { nextBounds.x += deltaX; nextBounds.y += deltaY; }\nif (state.mode.includes('e')) { ... }  // BUG: fires during move\n```\nAfter:\n```\nif (state.mode === 'move') {\n  nextBounds.x += deltaX;\n  nextBounds.y += deltaY;\n} else {\n  if (state.mode.includes('e')) { ... }\n  if (state.mode.includes('s')) { ... }\n  if (state.mode.includes('w')) { ... }\n  if (state.mode.includes('n')) { ... }\n}\n```",

  "meta_commentary": "**Execution order**: T1 \u2192 T2 and T3 (T2 and T3 are independent but both depend on T1 since they edit the same file) \u2192 T4 \u2192 T5 and T7 in parallel (T5 is OverlayEditor crop handles, T7 is VisualClip rendering \u2014 independent files) \u2192 T6 (depends on T5's overlay work) \u2192 T8 validates everything.\n\n**Highest-confidence fix**: T1 is mechanical and definitively proven \u2014 `'move'.includes('e')` === `true`. This alone resolves the user's #1 complaint (shape changes during move).\n\n**Ghost artifact**: The shadow is NOT a cleanup/leak issue. It's an intentionally rendered `dragOriginStyle` div (OverlayEditor.tsx:297-313) showing the clip's original bounds with a dashed border during drag. T3 simply removes it. The concept is reborn in T6 as the crop ghost.\n\n**Critical serialization gotcha** (from open flag `crop-persistence-misses-serialization-path`): timeline-data.ts has TWO separate places that manually enumerate clip fields \u2014 `getDefaultClipMeta` (load direction, line 101-123) and the serialization block (save direction, lines 234-256). Both iterate fields EXPLICITLY, not dynamically from the type. Adding crop to the interface alone does nothing; each mapping site must be updated. The serialize.ts whitelist is a third gate \u2014 it validates and rejects unknown keys.\n\n**Reset position**: The existing `handleResetClipPosition` (useTimelineEditing.ts:843-856) only clears x/y/width/height. Forgetting to also clear crop fields means \"Reset position\" leaves the clip visually cropped after position reset \u2014 a confusing UX bug.\n\n**Crop rendering approach**: `clip-path: inset()` on VisualClip.tsx is the simplest path that works in both Remotion preview and final render. No restructuring of the single-rectangle-per-clip model needed. The OverlayEditor's visual bounds must separately account for crop to show the cropped sub-region, while preserving the full x/y/width/height for the underlying clip area.\n\n**Edge handles vs corner handles**: Edge handles trigger crop modes, corner handles trigger resize modes. Both live inside the same overlay button element. The startDrag callback already accepts a `mode` parameter, so the dispatch is clean. Edge handles should have distinct cursors (ns-resize for top/bottom, ew-resize for left/right) to differentiate from corner resize cursors (nwse/nesw)."
}

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Fix the video editor's OverlayEditor (preview drag/resize) system. Three issues: (1) Moving a clip on the preview changes its aspect ratio/shape when it should maintain dimensions — only corner resize should change size. (2) Need edge dragging for cropping (left/right/top/bottom edges crop the clip) vs corner dragging for resize. (3) Ghost/shadow artifact when dragging clips on the timeline — something visual lingers until drop. Investigate whether these are symptomatic of poor structure in OverlayEditor.tsx and related code, or just incremental fixes. Goal: make preview overlay interactions predictable and clean — move preserves shape, corners resize, edges crop, no visual artifacts.

User notes and answers:
- T3/T4 were completely misdirected at timeline drag code. Refocus entirely on OverlayEditor.tsx preview interactions. The ghost is likely related to the drag preview rendering in OverlayEditor itself — possibly the live drag overlay (lines 327-359) or how bounds are rendered during drag. For crop UX: selected clip shows full uncropped bounds as a dimmed ghost behind the visible cropped area.

        Idea:
Fix the video editor's OverlayEditor (preview drag/resize) system. Three issues: (1) Moving a clip on the preview changes its aspect ratio/shape when it should maintain dimensions — only corner resize should change size. (2) Need edge dragging for cropping (left/right/top/bottom edges crop the clip) vs corner dragging for resize. (3) Ghost/shadow artifact when dragging clips on the timeline — something visual lingers until drop. Investigate whether these are symptomatic of poor structure in OverlayEditor.tsx and related code, or just incremental fixes. Goal: make preview overlay interactions predictable and clean — move preserves shape, corners resize, edges crop, no visual artifacts.

User notes and answers:
- T3/T4 were completely misdirected at timeline drag code. Refocus entirely on OverlayEditor.tsx preview interactions. The ghost is likely related to the drag preview rendering in OverlayEditor itself — possibly the live drag overlay (lines 327-359) or how bounds are rendered during drag. For crop UX: selected clip shows full uncropped bounds as a dimmed ghost behind the visible cropped area.

The next patch wires that through `OverlayEditor` and adds the matching `clip-path` on the Remotion side.

        Idea:
Fix the video editor's OverlayEditor (preview drag/resize) system. Three issues: (1) Moving a clip on the preview changes its aspect ratio/shape when it should maintain dimensions — only corner resize should change size. (2) Need edge dragging for cropping (left/right/top/bottom edges crop the clip) vs corner dragging for resize. (3) Ghost/shadow artifact when dragging clips on the timeline — something visual lingers until drop. Investigate whether these are symptomatic of poor structure in OverlayEditor.tsx and related code, or just incremental fixes. Goal: make preview overlay interactions predictable and clean — move preserves shape, corners resize, edges crop, no visual artifacts.

User notes and answers:
- T3/T4 were completely misdirected at timeline drag code. Refocus entirely on OverlayEditor.tsx preview interactions. The ghost is likely related to the drag preview rendering in OverlayEditor itself — possibly the live drag overlay (lines 327-359) or how bounds are rendered during drag. For crop UX: selected clip shows full uncropped bounds as a dimmed ghost behind the visible cropped area.

        Idea:
Fix the video editor's OverlayEditor (preview drag/resize) system. Three issues: (1) Moving a clip on the preview changes its aspect ratio/shape when it should maintain dimensions — only corner resize should change size. (2) Need edge dragging for cropping (left/right/top/bottom edges crop the clip) vs corner dragging for resize. (3) Ghost/shadow artifact when dragging clips on the timeline — something visual lingers until drop. Investigate whether these are symptomatic of poor structure in OverlayEditor.tsx and related code, or just incremental fixes. Goal: make preview overlay interactions predictable and clean — move preserves shape, corners resize, edges crop, no visual artifacts.

User notes and answers:
- T3/T4 were completely misdirected at timeline drag code. Refocus entirely on OverlayEditor.tsx preview interactions. The ghost is likely related to the drag preview rendering in OverlayEditor itself — possibly the live drag overlay (lines 327-359) or how bounds are rendered during drag. For crop UX: selected clip shows full uncropped bounds as a dimmed ghost behind the visible cropped area.

It's an intentionally rendered `dragOriginStyle` div (OverlayEditor.tsx:297-313) showing the clip's original bounds with a dashed border during drag. T3 simply removes it. The concept is reborn in T6 as the crop ghost.\n\n**Critical serialization gotcha** (from open flag `crop-persistence-misses-serialization-path`): timeline-data.ts has TWO separate places that manually enumerate clip fields \u2014 `getDefaultClipMeta` (load direction, line 101-123) and the serialization block (save direction, lines 234-256). Both iterate fields EXPLICITLY, not dynamically from the type. Adding crop to the interface alone does nothing; each mapping site must be updated. The serialize.ts whitelist is a third gate \u2014 it validates and rejects unknown keys.\n\n**Reset position**: The existing `handleResetClipPosition` (useTimelineEditing.ts:843-856) only clears x/y/width/height. Forgetting to also clear crop fields means \"Reset position\" leaves the clip visually cropped after position reset \u2014 a confusing UX bug.\n\n**Crop rendering approach**: `clip-path: inset()` on VisualClip.tsx is the simplest path that works in both Remotion preview and final render. No restructuring of the single-rectangle-per-clip model needed. The OverlayEditor's visual bounds must separately account for crop to show the cropped sub-region, while preserving the full x/y/width/height for the underlying clip area.\n\n**Edge handles vs corner handles**: Edge handles trigger crop modes, corner handles trigger resize modes. Both live inside the same overlay button element. The startDrag callback already accepts a `mode` parameter, so the dispatch is clean. Edge handles should have distinct cursors (ns-resize for top/bottom, ew-resize for left/right) to differentiate from corner resize cursors (nwse/nesw)."

        Idea:
Fix the video editor's OverlayEditor (preview drag/resize) system. Three issues: (1) Moving a clip on the preview changes its aspect ratio/shape when it should maintain dimensions — only corner resize should change size. (2) Need edge dragging for cropping (left/right/top/bottom edges crop the clip) vs corner dragging for resize. (3) Ghost/shadow artifact when dragging clips on the timeline — something visual lingers until drop. Investigate whether these are symptomatic of poor structure in OverlayEditor.tsx and related code, or just incremental fixes. Goal: make preview overlay interactions predictable and clean — move preserves shape, corners resize, edges crop, no visual artifacts.

User notes and answers:
- T3/T4 were completely misdirected at timeline drag code. Refocus entirely on OverlayEditor.tsx preview interactions. The ghost is likely related to the drag preview rendering in OverlayEditor itself — possibly the live drag overlay (lines 327-359) or how bounds are rendered during drag. For crop UX: selected clip shows full uncropped bounds as a dimmed ghost behind the visible cropped area.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Fix the video editor's OverlayEditor (preview drag/resize) system. Three issues: (1) Moving a clip on the preview changes its aspect ratio/shape when it should maintain dimensions — only corner resize should change size. (2) Need edge dragging for cropping (left/right/top/bottom edges crop the clip) vs corner dragging for resize. (3) Ghost/shadow artifact when dragging clips on the timeline — something visual lingers until drop. Investigate whether these are symptomatic of poor structure in OverlayEditor.tsx and related code, or just incremental fixes. Goal: make preview overlay interactions predictable and clean — move preserves shape, corners resize, edges crop, no visual artifacts.

User notes and answers:
- T3/T4 were completely misdirected at timeline drag code. Refocus entirely on OverlayEditor.tsx preview interactions. The ghost is likely related to the drag preview rendering in OverlayEditor itself — possibly the live drag overlay (lines 327-359) or how bounds are rendered during drag. For crop UX: selected clip shows full uncropped bounds as a dimmed ghost behind the visible cropped area.

Edge crop \u2014 dragging an edge inward visually crops from that side; edges only on non-text clips.\n5. Selected cropped clip shows dimmed ghost of full uncropped bounds; deselected shows only cropped area.\n6. Crop persists after save/load \u2014 inspect serialized JSON to verify cropTop/cropRight/cropBottom/cropLeft round-trip.\n7. 'Reset position' button clears both position and crop values.\n8.     "description": "Fix string-matching bug in OverlayEditor.tsx drag handler (lines 215-232). Fix: wrap lines 219-232 in an `else` block so resize logic only runs when `state.mode !== 'move'`.\n\nFile: `src/tools/video-editor/components/PreviewPanel/OverlayEditor.tsx`\n\nBefore:\n```\nif (state.mode === 'move') { nextBounds.x += deltaX; nextBounds.y += deltaY; }\nif (state.mode.includes('e')) { ... }  // BUG: fires during move\n```\nAfter:\n```\nif (state.mode === 'move') {\n  nextBounds.x += deltaX;\n  nextBounds.y += deltaY;\n} else {\n  if (state.mode.includes('e')) { ... }\n  if (state.mode.includes('s')) { ... }\n  if (state.mode.includes('w')) { ... }\n  if (state.mode.includes('n')) { ... }\n}\n```",
    "depends_on": [],
    "status": "done",
    "executor_notes": "Wrapped the directional resize checks in an `else` after the `move` branch in `OverlayEditor.tsx`, so move drags now update only `x` and `y`. Verified the `e/s/w/n` `includes()` branches are all guarded by that `else`, preventing width/height changes during move.",

Screen-pixel deltas (`event.clientX - startMouseX`) are applied directly to composition-space coordinates, so move/resize speed doesn't match cursor when preview is scaled.\n\nFile: `src/tools/video-editor/components/PreviewPanel/OverlayEditor.tsx`\n\n1. Add `scaleX: number` and `scaleY: number` to the dragState ref type (lines 47-55).\n2. In `startDrag` (lines 249-262), compute and store: `scaleX = compositionWidth / layout.width`, `scaleY = compositionHeight / layout.height`. Use current `layout` state value (captured at drag start to avoid stale closures).\n3. In `onMouseMove` (lines 211-212), multiply deltas: `const deltaX = (event.clientX - state.startMouseX) * state.scaleX;` and same for deltaY.\n\nNote: `layout` is React state; storing the scale at drag start in the ref avoids closure staleness.",

  "tasks": [
    {
      "id": "T1",
      "description": "Fix string-matching bug in OverlayEditor.tsx drag handler (lines 215-232). The `state.mode.includes('e')` checks run unconditionally after the `move` branch, and `'move'.includes('e')` is `true`, causing every move to also apply east-resize. Fix: wrap lines 219-232 in an `else` block so resize logic only runs when `state.mode !== 'move'`.\n\nFile: `src/tools/video-editor/components/PreviewPanel/OverlayEditor.tsx`\n\nBefore:\n```\nif (state.mode === 'move') { nextBounds.x += deltaX; nextBounds.y += deltaY; }\nif (state.mode.includes('e')) { ... }  // BUG: fires during move\n```\nAfter:\n```\nif (state.mode === 'move') {\n  nextBounds.x += deltaX;\n  nextBounds.y += deltaY;\n} else {\n  if (state.mode.includes('e')) { ... }\n  if (state.mode.includes('s')) { ... }\n  if (state.mode.includes('w')) { ... }\n  if (state.mode.includes('n')) { ... }\n}\n```",
      "depends_on": [],
      "status": "done",
      "executor_notes": "Wrapped the directional resize checks in an `else` after the `move` branch in `OverlayEditor.tsx`, so move drags now update only `x` and `y`. Verified the `e/s/w/n` `includes()` branches are all guarded by that `else`, preventing width/height changes during move.",

        Idea:
Fix the video editor's OverlayEditor (preview drag/resize) system. Three issues: (1) Moving a clip on the preview changes its aspect ratio/shape when it should maintain dimensions — only corner resize should change size. (2) Need edge dragging for cropping (left/right/top/bottom edges crop the clip) vs corner dragging for resize. (3) Ghost/shadow artifact when dragging clips on the timeline — something visual lingers until drop. Investigate whether these are symptomatic of poor structure in OverlayEditor.tsx and related code, or just incremental fixes. Goal: make preview overlay interactions predictable and clean — move preserves shape, corners resize, edges crop, no visual artifacts.

User notes and answers:
- T3/T4 were completely misdirected at timeline drag code. Refocus entirely on OverlayEditor.tsx preview interactions. The ghost is likely related to the drag preview rendering in OverlayEditor itself — possibly the live drag overlay (lines 327-359) or how bounds are rendered during drag. For crop UX: selected clip shows full uncropped bounds as a dimmed ghost behind the visible cropped area.

    {
      "id": "T1",
      "description": "Fix string-matching bug in OverlayEditor.tsx drag handler (lines 215-232). The `state.mode.includes('e')` checks run unconditionally after the `move` branch, and `'move'.includes('e')` is `true`, causing every move to also apply east-resize. Fix: wrap lines 219-232 in an `else` block so resize logic only runs when `state.mode !== 'move'`.\n\nFile: `src/tools/video-editor/components/PreviewPanel/OverlayEditor.tsx`\n\nBefore:\n```\nif (state.mode === 'move') { nextBounds.x += deltaX; nextBounds.y += deltaY; }\nif (state.mode.includes('e')) { ... }  // BUG: fires during move\n```\nAfter:\n```\nif (state.mode === 'move') {\n  nextBounds.x += deltaX;\n  nextBounds.y += deltaY;\n} else {\n  if (state.mode.includes('e')) { ... }\n  if (state.mode.includes('s')) { ... }\n  if (state.mode.includes('w')) { ... }\n  if (state.mode.includes('n')) { ... }\n}\n```",
      "depends_on": [],
      "status": "done",
      "executor_notes": "Wrapped the directional resize checks in an `else` after the `move` branch in `OverlayEditor.tsx`, so move drags now update only `x` and `y`. Verified the `e/s/w/n` `includes()` branches are all guarded by that `else`, preventing width/height changes during move.",

      "description": "Fix coordinate-space mismatch in OverlayEditor.tsx. Screen-pixel deltas (`event.clientX - startMouseX`) are applied directly to composition-space coordinates, so move/resize speed doesn't match cursor when preview is scaled.\n\nFile: `src/tools/video-editor/components/PreviewPanel/OverlayEditor.tsx`\n\n1. Add `scaleX: number` and `scaleY: number` to the dragState ref type (lines 47-55).\n2. In `startDrag` (lines 249-262), compute and store: `scaleX = compositionWidth / layout.width`, `scaleY = compositionHeight / layout.height`. Use current `layout` state value (captured at drag start to avoid stale closures).\n3. In `onMouseMove` (lines 211-212), multiply deltas: `const deltaX = (event.clientX - state.startMouseX) * state.scaleX;` and same for deltaY.\n\nNote: `layout` is React state; storing the scale at drag start in the ref avoids closure staleness.",

Forgetting to also clear crop fields means \"Reset position\" leaves the clip visually cropped after position reset \u2014 a confusing UX bug.\n\n**Crop rendering approach**: `clip-path: inset()` on VisualClip.tsx is the simplest path that works in both Remotion preview and final render. No restructuring of the single-rectangle-per-clip model needed. The OverlayEditor's visual bounds must separately account for crop to show the cropped sub-region, while preserving the full x/y/width/height for the underlying clip area.\n\n**Edge handles vs corner handles**: Edge handles trigger crop modes, corner handles trigger resize modes. Both live inside the same overlay button element. The startDrag callback already accepts a `mode` parameter, so the dispatch is clean. Edge handles should have distinct cursors (ns-resize for top/bottom, ew-resize for left/right) to differentiate from corner resize cursors (nwse/nesw)."
